- 2026-05-20 · 6.9 + 6.10 + 6.11 · Phase 3 analytics engine batch 2 of 2 landed (no UI, no migration). **6.9** `cashflowForecast()` (forecast.js, replaces stub) projects end-of-current-month spend via equal-weighted ensemble of three models: linear-weighted least-squares with `α^(daysElapsed - x)` decay (α=0.95) + ≤ 2-point flat-extrapolation fallback + `projected = max(projected, currentSpend, 0)` clamp; historical-month with 3-tier fallback (same-MM across years → last 3 complete months → lifetime average month, all excluding current month so the model never trains on its own truth); rolling-90d at `(sum / window) × daysInMonth` with `min(90, history_days)` thin-history window. Confidence: `max/min ≤ 1.10 → high`, `≤ 1.25 → medium`, else `low`; zero-min → low. Returns `{ ready, asof_month_key, days_elapsed, days_in_month, current_spend, history_days, models, ensemble, range: { min, max }, confidence }`. Gates: `no_expenses | insufficient_history | insufficient_month | models_unresolved`. Cache TTL 6h, scope FORECAST. `approxNormalCDF()` deferred to 5.A.02. **6.10** `seasonalCalendar({months=36})` + `dayOfWeekPattern({months=12})` + `dayOfMonthHistogram({months=12})` in seasonal.js (replaces stub). seasonalCalendar rolls (year, month_key) → SUM(amount) then averages across years; pads to 12 cells with `avg_spend: null` on miss; gates `not_enough_months` when distinct months < 12. dayOfWeekPattern uses `strftime('%w', ...)` (0=Sun); avg_spend per dow divides by distinct dow-dates (not txn count) so Saturdays show as per-Saturday averages, not per-transaction. dayOfMonthHistogram uses `strftime('%d', ...)`; both pad to full 7 / 31 cells. All three 24h TTL, scope SEASONAL. **6.11** `categoryVarianceMatrix({months=6})` in new variance.js. Reads monthly_summary LEFT JOIN categories (deleted_at IS NULL); zero-pads missing months across the window; computes Bessel-corrected (n−1) stddev so spreadsheet-savvy users see expected values; cv = stddev / mean (dimensionless, drives 6.19's heatmap colour intensity); single-month series → stddev null, cv null. Soft-deleted-category rows fall through `COALESCE(c.name, ...)` to "Uncategorised". Categories sorted total desc. Cache TTL 24h, scope SPEND. **app/src/analytics/index.js** gains 5 new exports (cashflowForecast, seasonalCalendar, dayOfWeekPattern, dayOfMonthHistogram, categoryVarianceMatrix). Validation **41/41**: `/tmp/drift_6_9_forecast_pure.mjs` 18/18 (6 linear-regression assertions incl. flat=exact, rising>flat, ≤2-point fallback, ≥-currentSpend clamp, empty→0, recent-day bias; 5 ensemble assertions incl. confidence boundaries at 1.10 and 1.25; 7 SQL assertions via node:sqlite against a 90-day synthetic series), `/tmp/drift_6_10_seasonal_validate.mjs` 10/10 (24-month fixture with July/Sat/dom-1 bumps; July avg ≫ Jan, Sat avg ≫ Wed, dom1 avg > dom15; cells padded to 12/7/31; gates), `/tmp/drift_6_11_variance_validate.mjs` 13/13 (Food cv≈0.47, Bills stddev=0 perfectly stable, Travel zero-padded single-month, id=0 sentinel + soft-deleted-cat falls to Uncategorised label, total-desc sort, max_month/min_month). Babel parse-check passes on all 4 touched files. **No schema migration**, **no native dep**, **no native rebuild**. Files touched: `app/src/analytics/forecast.js` (replace stub), `app/src/analytics/seasonal.js` (replace stub), `app/src/analytics/variance.js` (new), `app/src/analytics/index.js` (5 added re-exports), `/tmp/drift_6_9_forecast_pure.mjs` + `/tmp/drift_6_10_seasonal_validate.mjs` + `/tmp/drift_6_11_variance_validate.mjs` (harnesses, not committed). No commit. Phase 3 reaches **11/22**; overall **131/187 = 70.05%** · uncommitted · `app/src/analytics/{forecast.js,seasonal.js,variance.js,index.js}`

- 2026-05-21 · 7.2 · Subscription calendar — Phase 4 at 2/15. **No schema migration, no new dependency, no native rebuild.** The "3-day-before reminder schedule" half of the tracker line is recorded as closed-by-7.1 in the Decision log; 7.2 ships only the calendar screen. New pure-function module `app/src/features/subs/projection.js` exports `nextOccurrencesFrom(nextBillStr, period, horizonStart, horizonEnd) → Date[]` (reuses `periodToMonthly()` from `analytics/subscriptions.js` for the period vocabulary; applies Excel-EDATE-style anchor-day clamping so Jan-31 monthly walks Jan-31/Feb-28/29/Mar-31/Apr-30 instead of drifting; weekly/daily step by literal day arithmetic; yearly Feb-29 clamps to Feb-28 in non-leap years; quarterly = +3 months; unknown period falls back to monthly per the established 6.8 convention; hard cap `MAX_OCCURRENCES = 60` so a pathological period='d' over a wide horizon can't blow up; past-anchor catch-up walks forward until first occurrence is ≥ horizonStart so users with stale `next_bill` still see correct upcoming dates), `projectSubsForMonth(subs, monthKey) → {subsByDay: Map<day, {sub, date}[]>, dailySubs: sub[], skippedSubs: sub[]}` (defensively filters cancelled + deleted + null next_bill; routes daily-period subs to `dailySubs` to keep the grid readable; fans monthly/weekly/yearly/quarterly into `subsByDay`), `monthGridCells(year, monthIndex) → {leadingBlanks, daysInMonth, cells: 42-entry array}` (Sun..Sat aligned, always exactly 42 cells), plus `monthKeyOf`, `shiftMonth`, `SUB_CAL_MONTH_NAMES`, `SUB_CAL_WEEKDAY_HEAD` helpers. New screen `app/src/features/subs/screens/SubCalendar.js` — hero card with month total + bill count; 5-cell PagerStrip with `‹` / `›` (clamped at MAX_PAGE_BACK=6 / MAX_PAGE_FORWARD=12 months, slides so the selected offset is roughly centred); MonthGrid renders 7-col weekday header + 6×7 day cells, each cell shows day number + up to 3 dot indicators coloured from `sub.color` + "+N" overflow when ≥4 subs share a day, today gets a 1.5px coral ring border, selected day gets coral fill + white day text; SelectionCallout shows "{N} due on {Mon} {day}" with `sym{totalAmount}` + per-sub rows (icon, name, period, next_bill — tap routes to EditSub); Footnotes section for daily subs (inline list with names + amounts, "Not shown in the grid to keep it readable") + null-next-bill subs (tappable card that deep-links to Subs); auto-selects today on mount when offset=0 and clears when pager moves away; empty-subs state with 🗓️ glyph. Uses per-feature hooks `useTheme()` + `useSubs()` + `useSettings()` (no `useApp()` — matches CLAUDE.md "new code should import the per-feature hook directly"). New nav route `SubCalendar` registered in `app/src/navigation/index.js` (import + withBoundary + Stack.Screen "Subscription calendar"). Entry-point additions: `Subs.js` gets a "View calendar →" link below the hero numbers (rendered only when there's ≥1 sub); `analytics/screens/Hub.js` Reports section gets a 🗓️ "Subscription calendar" row immediately below the existing 🔁 "Subscriptions" row. Validation: `/tmp/drift_7_2_validate.mjs` 30/30 — 12 projection axes (monthly normal, month-end clamp, leap-year clamp, weekly, yearly normal, yearly Feb-29 clamp, quarterly, past-anchor catch-up, null next_bill, empty horizon, unknown→monthly fallback, daily MAX_OCCURRENCES cap at 60), 7 projectSubsForMonth axes (same-day grouping, monthly+weekly mixed, daily routing to footnote, cancelled+deleted+null filter, Jan-31→Feb-28 cross-month projection), 11 monthGridCells axes across 3 months (May 2026 Fri-start, Feb 2024 leap Thu-start, Feb 2025 non-leap Sat-start — each asserts leadingBlanks + daysInMonth + 42-cell total + in-month count). Per CLAUDE.md "extension-less imports from app/src/ won't resolve outside Metro" the harness inlines the pure-function logic verbatim (projection.js imports from `analytics/subscriptions.js` which imports `from '../db'`, so the real module can't be loaded from Node). Manual on-device UI verification (tap empty day, tap day with subs, pager edge limits, today auto-highlight on mount, monthly sub crossing month boundary, daily-subs footnote rendering) **not run** this session — needs `npm start` + device; punch list in the Decision log. - 2026-05-21 · 7.3 · Tags + UI in Add/EditExpense + filter integration — Phase 4 at 3/15. **No new dependency, no native rebuild.** Schema v29 adds `tags` (id, name COLLATE NOCASE, color, created_at, deleted_at) with partial UNIQUE `idx_tags_name_live` on `name COLLATE NOCASE WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` so soft-deleting a tag frees its name for re-use; `expense_tags` (composite PK (expense_id, tag_id), FK CASCADE both directions, created_at) + reverse index `idx_expense_tags_tag` to cover the filter subquery `WHERE tag_id IN (?)` (the composite PK already covers `WHERE expense_id = ?`). New feature folder `app/src/features/tags/`: `repo.js` exports `tagsRepo.{listLive, get, findByNameLive, getOrCreate, listForExpense, setForExpense, rename, remove, restore}` — `listLive` does LEFT JOIN expense_tags GROUP BY tag id with `usage_count` so the chip surface can sort most-used-first without a separate counter column; `getOrCreate` is idempotent (returns existing live row if found NOCASE, else INSERTs); `setForExpense(expenseId, names)` resolves names to ids outside the transaction (so lastInsertRowId stays deterministic on new tags) then diffs desired-vs-existing and runs INSERT/DELETE inside a single `withTransactionAsync`; `rename(id, newName)` silently merges when newName collides with another live tag — moves joins via `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO expense_tags (expense_id, tag_id) SELECT expense_id, dest_id FROM expense_tags WHERE tag_id = src_id`, deletes source joins, soft-deletes source; `remove` soft-deletes (joins retained for restore). `context.js` — TagsProvider holds `tags[]` + ready flag, exposes getOrCreateTag/renameTag/removeTag/restoreTag, registers `tags` against RefreshBus. `components/TagChipSurface.js` — shared between Add and EditExpense: horizontal-scroll chip row capped at top-12-by-usage with extra-selected tags pinned so the user's selection never falls off-screen, "+ Add" reveals a TextInput (autoCapitalize=none, autoCorrect=false, autoFocus, maxLength=24, returnKeyType=done), commitNewTag calls getOrCreateTag and adds the new name to the selection. `screens/ManageTags.js` — list of every live tag with hero card + per-row inline-rename TextInput + SwipeableRow delete; rename-merge fires Alert "Tag 'X' already exists. Renaming will merge … {usage_count} expenses re-tagged. Continue?" before calling repo.rename; delete fires Alert with explicit "Unlinks but doesn't delete expenses" copy + Toast with Undo (restore). TagsProvider slotted between CategoriesProvider and ItemsProvider in AppRoot (both independent reference slices); `useTags().ready` added to ReadyGate. ExpensesProvider's 4 writers (addExpense / updateExpense / addExpenseWithItems / updateExpenseWithItems) now destructure `{ tags, ...rest }` and call `tagsRepo.setForExpense(id, tags)` when `tags` is an array (including `[]` to clear); when `tags` is `undefined` the joins are untouched — every existing caller stays backward-compatible. Context value exposes `tagsForExpense(id)` async reader for Edit/Detail. `filters.js` adds `'tagIds'` to `CRITERIA_KEYS`; `buildWhere` emits `e.id IN (SELECT expense_id FROM expense_tags WHERE tag_id IN (?, ?))` (OR semantics, indexed via idx_expense_tags_tag); `hasActiveFilters` adds the tagIds branch; `criteriaToHumanLabel` accepts new `tagMap` arg and renders "#name" for 1 tag or "N tags" for multiple. FilterSheet imports `useTags`, computes `tagMap` (Object.fromEntries), threads it through criteriaToHumanLabel's openSaveSheet call, adds `toggleTag` and new `<Section title="Tags">` after Payment using the existing `<ChipRow wrap>` + `<Chip>` primitives — chips render `#tagname` with the established coral-active style; empty state shows "No tags yet — add one when creating or editing an expense." Add.js gets `useTags()` + `TagChipSurface` rendered between the carbon/recurring row and the keypad (visible only in expense mode); both quick and detailed save paths thread `tags: tagNames`. EditExpense.js seeds tagNames from `tagsForExpense(e.id)` in a new useEffect on mount, renders `TagChipSurface` between Recurring and Notes, threads tags into both update paths. New nav route `ManageTags` registered in `navigation/index.js` (import + withBoundary + Stack.Screen "Manage tags"). - 2026-05-21 · 7.5 · EMI tracking — Phase 4 at 5/15. **No new dependency, no native rebuild.** Schema v30 adds `emi_loans` (id, name, lender NULL, principal REAL, annual_rate_pct REAL, tenure_months INTEGER, start_date YYYY-MM-DD, installments_paid INTEGER DEFAULT 0, emi_override REAL NULL, bill_day INTEGER DEFAULT 1 CHECK 1..28, notes NULL, icon DEFAULT '🏦', color DEFAULT '#888', created_at, deleted_at) + partial `idx_emi_loans_live` on `created_at DESC WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. ALTER TABLE expenses ADD COLUMN `emi_loan_id INTEGER REFERENCES emi_loans(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` + partial `idx_expenses_emi` on `emi_loan_id WHERE emi_loan_id IS NOT NULL`. `bill_day` CHECK capped at 28 to dodge Feb edge cases. `emi_override` lets the user pin the bank's actual EMI when the reducing-balance formula's rounding drifts (load-bearing per risk register line 842 — Indian banks differ by ₹0.01–₹2.00 per installment). FK ON DELETE SET NULL matches the convention for `category_id`/`account_id`/`trip_id` (line 32/188/190) — deleting the loan doesn't orphan-delete payment expenses, just unlinks them. `emi_loans` appended to TABLES *after* expenses + income so resetAll's children-first wipe order keeps the FK SET NULL trigger a no-op (children are already gone). V1_REQUIRED_TABLES untouched. New feature folder `app/src/features/emi/`: `amortization.js` (pure JS, no DB/RN imports — exports `computeEMI(P, annualRatePct, n)` using standard reducing-balance `EMI = P × r × (1+r)^n / ((1+r)^n − 1)` with `r = annual / 12 / 100`, P/n fallback for zero rate, returns 0 on invalid input; `buildSchedule({principal, annualRatePct, tenureMonths, startDate, billDay, override?})` walks N installments via anchor-day clamping (so Jan-31 monthly schedule becomes Jan-31/Feb-28/Mar-31/Apr-30 cleanly), last installment adjusts payment so `closing_balance` lands at exactly 0 regardless of which EMI value was used (computed or override) — mop-up for cumulative rounding drift; `projectState(loan, {asOf})` derives `{emiAmount, schedule, installmentsPaid, installmentsRemaining, outstandingPrincipal, nextDueDate, totalInterest, totalPaid}` from loan terms + user-controlled `installments_paid` — outstanding = closing_balance after that installment, nextDueDate = the dueDate of installment #(installmentsPaid+1) or null when fully paid; `tenureLabel(months)` produces "6 months" / "1 year" / "2y 3mo" for the EditEMI sub-label), `repo.js` (listLive sorted by created_at DESC; get/create/update/remove/restore soft-delete-aware; `linkedExpenses(loanId)` returns the live expense rows tagged with that emi_loan_id ordered by expense_date DESC; bulk `linkedCountsAll()` returns `{[loanId]: count}` so the EMI list renders "X linked" without per-row queries; auto-assigns icon/color from a default palette indexed by COUNT(*) when caller doesn't specify), `context.js` (EmiProvider holds `loans[]` + `linkedCounts{}`, registers `emi` against RefreshBus; exposes loans/addLoan/updateLoan/removeLoan/restoreLoan/linkedExpenses), `screens/EMI.js` (hero: total monthly EMI + loan count + total outstanding; per-loan rows with icon circle / name / lender / rate × tenure / "X of N paid · Y linked · ₹Z left" / progress bar / monthly EMI right-aligned + "next YYYY-MM-DD" sub; SwipeableRow swipe-delete with Undo Toast; long-press = same; FAB → EditEMI(create); empty state with 🏦 glyph), `screens/EditEMI.js` (full form: name + optional lender + 8-icon picker + 8-colour picker + principal + rate + tenure with live "X years Y months" hint + start_date YYYY-MM-DD + bill_day stepper + installments_paid + EMI override field with live `Computed ₹X` hint + `drift ±₹Y` display when override is set + notes; below the form a "SCHEDULE PREVIEW" cream card showing the computed EMI + total interest + total paid + next-3-installments table — gives the user feedback that the math works before saving; Save button writes via addLoan/updateLoan; Delete button on edit-mode fires Alert-confirmed soft-delete + Undo Toast). EmiProvider slotted between SubsProvider and GoalsProvider in AppRoot (same scheduled-payment family, no cross-deps on Categories/Items); `useEmi().ready` added to ReadyGate. Expenses repo updated: `emi_loan_id` threaded through `create()`, `update()`, and `createWithItems()` SQL column lists + param arrays. EditExpense.js imports `useEmi` and renders an "EMI PAYMENT" chip row card (between TagChipSurface and Notes, gated on `emiLoans.length > 0`) — chips show `{loan.icon} {loan.name}`, tap toggles `emiLoanId` (sets or clears); both updateExpense and updateExpenseWithItems patches carry `emi_loan_id: emiLoanId`. ExpensesProvider already destructures arbitrary patch keys via `{...rest}`, so the only context change required was the repo's column list. Hub Reports gets 🏦 "EMIs & loans · Amortization schedule + outstanding balance" row immediately below the existing 🗓️ "Subscription calendar" row. Profile "More" section gets 🏦 "Manage EMIs · Track loans with amortization" row immediately below the existing 🏷️ "Manage tags" row. - 2026-05-23 · 7.7 · Pantry — Phase 4 at 7/15. **No new dependency, no native rebuild.** Schema v32 (name `pantry-items`) adds `pantry_items` (id, normalized_name TEXT NOT NULL, display_name TEXT NOT NULL, kind DEFAULT 'other', canonical_unit DEFAULT 'pcs', current_qty REAL DEFAULT 0, reorder_threshold REAL NULL, target_qty REAL NULL, last_topped_up_at TEXT NULL, notes TEXT NULL, icon TEXT NULL, created_at, deleted_at) + partial `uq_pantry_normalized_name_live` UNIQUE on `normalized_name WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (one live row per canonical item; soft-deleted history coexists with a re-created live row) + partial `idx_pantry_low_stock` on `current_qty WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND reorder_threshold IS NOT NULL` (covers the shopping-list + low-stock notification scan exactly). `pantry_items` appended to TABLES near the other user-customisation tables (no FK; ordering organisational). V1_REQUIRED_TABLES untouched. Auto-populate: `expRepo.createWithItems` imports `pantryRepo` and calls `pantryRepo.autoPopulateFromItems(items)` AFTER the createWithItems transaction commits, wrapped in try/catch so a pantry write failure cannot unwind the expense save. The gating signal is `item_summary.points_count >= 2` (matches reorderQueue convention — single-shot purchases skipped). Inside its own transaction the function: looks up item_summary by normalized_name, reads points_count + display_name + kind + canonical_unit; if `points_count < 2` skips; if a live pantry_items row exists for the normalized_name increments current_qty by canonical_qty + stamps last_topped_up_at; else INSERTs a new row copying display_name/kind/canonical_unit from item_summary. New feature folder `app/src/features/pantry/`: `repo.js` (vehiclesRepo-style surface: listLive sorted by display_name COLLATE NOCASE; listLowStock with composite-ratio sort; get/getByName; create with last_topped_up_at stamped at INSERT; update; setQty clamped at 0 floor; incrementQty clamped at 0; remove/restore soft-delete; autoPopulateFromItems atomic loop; candidates query — items the user has bought but doesn't yet have a live pantry row for, used by the EditPantryItem picker), `context.js` (PantryProvider holds items[] + lowStock[]; refreshes on NotifyBus EXPENSE_CHANGED so the auto-populate result lands without explicit cross-context wiring; emits a new PANTRY_CHANGED event on every mutation), `screens/Pantry.js` (hero card showing tracked count + lowStock count; segmented two-tab control [Inventory · N] [Shopping · N]; PantryRow component renders icon circle + display_name + current qty + threshold sub-line tinted coral when below threshold; per-row -/+ stepper with step=0.1 for g/ml/kg/L and 1 for pcs; Inventory tab has a "Used up" button that sets qty=0; Shopping tab has a "Bought" button that restocks to target_qty when set else 2× threshold; SwipeableRow + long-press soft-delete with Undo Toast; tap → EditPantryItem; FAB → EditPantryItem; per-tab empty state with 🥗/🛒 glyph), `screens/EditPantryItem.js` (create flow: optional QUICK PICK candidate strip lists 10 top item_summary candidates the user hasn't yet tracked, tapping pre-fills display_name + canonical_unit + kind + normalized_name; or type a name from scratch which gets normalised via `normalizeName(displayName).normalized_name`; UNIT chip group [g/kg/ml/L/pcs]; current_qty + reorder_threshold + target_qty NumericInputs; ICON picker; NOTES; edit flow: hydrates from existing row, NAME field disabled when editing because normalized_name is stable post-creation, separate DISPLAY NAME field allows label changes; Delete button on edit-mode fires Alert-confirmed soft-delete + Undo Toast). PantryProvider slotted between FuelProvider and GoalsProvider in AppRoot — must come BEFORE NotificationsProvider (which mounts at the end) because the notifications context now imports usePantry; `usePantry().ready` added to ReadyGate. New nav routes `Pantry` (title "Pantry") + `EditPantryItem` (dynamic title). Hub Reports gets 🥗 "Pantry · Inventory + low-stock shopping list" row immediately below the existing ⛽ "Vehicles & fuel" row. Profile "More" section gets 🥗 "Manage pantry · Track what you own and what's running low" row immediately below "Manage vehicles". Notifications: NotifyBus event constants gain `PANTRY_CHANGED = 'pantry:changed'`. New `evaluatePantryLowStock({ pantry, settings, now? })` pure function in `notifications/checkers.js`: skips when notifications_enabled is false; iterates pantry rows; skips rows with deleted_at, NULL threshold, or threshold ≤ 0; emits a plan item when current_qty ≤ threshold; dedupe key `pantry:<normalized_name>:YYYY-Www` (ISO-8601 week, computed by Thursday-of-week + epoch days math) so a single item fires at most once per week regardless of how many save events land. - 2026-05-23 · 7.6 · Fuel & vehicle tracking — Phase 4 at 6/15. **No new dependency, no native rebuild.** Schema v31 (name `vehicles-and-fuel-fillups`) adds two tables. `vehicles` (id, name NOT NULL, type DEFAULT 'car' CHECK IN car/bike/scooter/other, fuel_type DEFAULT 'Petrol' CHECK IN Petrol/Diesel/CNG/Electric, registration_number NULL, notes NULL, icon DEFAULT '🚗', color DEFAULT '#888', created_at, deleted_at) + partial `idx_vehicles_live` on `created_at DESC WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. `fuel_fillups` (id, vehicle_id NOT NULL FK CASCADE → vehicles, expense_id NOT NULL UNIQUE FK CASCADE → expenses, fill_date TEXT NOT NULL, liters REAL NOT NULL, rate_per_l REAL NULL, amount REAL NOT NULL (denormalised mirror of expenses.amount — avoids JOIN on the per-vehicle hero card; both rows written in one transaction so they can't drift), odometer_km REAL NULL, is_full_tank INTEGER DEFAULT 1 CHECK IN (0,1), fuel_type TEXT NULL CHECK same enum or NULL — NULL means "use vehicle default", non-null overrides per-fill-up for bi-fuel cars, notes NULL, created_at, deleted_at) + partial `idx_fillups_vehicle_date` on `(vehicle_id, fill_date DESC) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` + `idx_fillups_expense` on `expense_id`. UNIQUE on `expense_id` enforces the 1-to-1 model decided at plan time — one fuel receipt = one fill-up row. `fuel_fillups` appended to TABLES *before* expenses (child-first wipe order; double-parent: both vehicles and expenses CASCADE on hard delete, so it's drained first); `vehicles` appended *after* expenses so its CASCADE-to-fillups trigger fires against an already-empty fillups table. V1_REQUIRED_TABLES untouched. New feature folder `app/src/features/fuel/`: `repo.js` exports `vehiclesRepo` (listLive/get/create/update/remove/restore — soft-delete-aware; auto-assigns icon/color from a 7×7 palette indexed by COUNT(*) when caller doesn't pass them) and `fillupsRepo` (listByVehicle/get/getByExpense/lastVehicleUsed/aggregatesByVehicle returning `{[vid]: {count, last_fill_date, this_month_spend}}` from a single GROUP BY against the partial index/mileageWindow which pulls the latest two `is_full_tank=1 AND odometer_km IS NOT NULL` rows ORDER BY fill_date DESC and returns `{km_driven, liters_burned, kmpl}` else null/createWithExpense which is the atomic dual-write — opens its own `withTransactionAsync`, resolves merchant_id via merchants.resolve, INSERTs the expense (full 21-column form matching expRepo.createWithItems so receipt_path/receipt_hash/tax-invoice fields/payment_method all flow through), captures `lastInsertRowId` for the fillup's `expense_id`, INSERTs the fillup, returns `{expense_id, fillup_id}`; if either INSERT fails the transaction rolls both back/updatePair which patches both rows in one transaction/remove which soft-deletes the fillup AND its linked expense AND its receipt_items in one transaction (symmetric to expRepo.remove which cascades the soft-delete to children)/restore which reverses the three soft-deletes). `context.js` (FuelProvider holds `vehicles[]` + `aggregates{}` only — per-vehicle fill-up lists are NOT eagerly held; VehicleDetail.js lazy-loads via listByVehicle on mount + on refresh, keeps in-memory footprint bounded as fill-up history grows. addFillup/updateFillup/removeFillup also call `useExpenses().refreshSummary` + `useExpenses().refresh()` so monthly_summary-backed pots and the 500-row expenses slice both pick up the change). 4 new screens: `screens/Vehicles.js` (hero card with this-month fuel spend totalled across vehicles + count; per-row icon circle / name / registration_number-or-type+fuel-type / last fill date / per-row this-month-spend + fillup count; SwipeableRow + long-press soft-delete with Undo Toast; FAB → EditVehicle; empty state with 🚗 glyph), `screens/EditVehicle.js` (form: name + 4-type chip + 4-fuel-type chip + registration_number + 8-icon picker + 8-colour picker + notes; Delete button on edit-mode fires Alert-confirmed soft-delete + Undo Toast), `screens/VehicleDetail.js` (header strip: icon/name/type+fuel+reg + Edit pill linking to EditVehicle; 2×2 hero stats grid showing THIS MONTH spend / LAST FILL date / ODOMETER km / MILEAGE km/L; FILL-UP HISTORY FlatList sorted descending by fill_date with per-row date + liters + rate + fuel_type + partial/odometer + amount; SwipeableRow swipe-delete + Undo Toast; tap row → EditFillup with id+vehicleId params; FAB → EditFillup with vehicleId param; pull-to-refresh; empty state with ⛽ glyph), `screens/EditFillup.js` (manual log path or hydrated edit path; form: vehicle chip group / pump merchant / category chip group / fill-date via DateTimePicker / liters / rate_per_l / amount auto-computed from liters×rate when both present AND user hasn't manually edited the amount field / odometer / full-tank Switch / 4-fuel-type chip group defaulting to vehicle's fuel_type / notes; dynamic-imports `@features/fuel/repo` for the get-by-id hydrate so VehicleDetail's fill-up lists don't need to live in context). FuelProvider slotted between EmiProvider and GoalsProvider in AppRoot; `useFuel().ready` added to ReadyGate. 4 new nav routes: `Vehicles` ("Vehicles & fuel" title) + `VehicleDetail` ("Vehicle") + `EditVehicle` (dynamic title) + `EditFillup` (dynamic title). Hub Reports gets ⛽ "Vehicles & fuel · Per-vehicle fill-up history + mileage" row immediately below the existing 🏦 "EMIs & loans" row. Profile "More" section gets ⛽ "Manage vehicles · Cars, bikes, and fuel history" row immediately below "Manage EMIs". Scan integration: `Scan.js` imports `useFuel`, holds new `format` + `vehicleId` + `odometerKm` state cleared in resetScreen; processImage captures `result.format` and auto-picks a vehicle for the chip (lastVehicleUsed() if any live fill-up exists, else vehicles[0]) only when format === 'fuel' AND vehicles.length > 0; review screen renders a "VEHICLE" horizontal chip strip (with explicit "None" pill to opt out of linkage) and an "ODOMETER (KM, OPTIONAL)" inline TextInput when a vehicle is selected, both inside the existing cream header card just above SAVE TO CATEGORY; performSave branches: when format === 'fuel' AND vehicleId != null it calls `addFillup({expense: expenseWithStorage, fillup})` with `liters = validItems[0].qty`, `rate_per_l = validItems[0].unit_price`, `amount = expense.amount`, `fuel_type` derived from `validItems[0].name` via a 4-way regex (Diesel/CNG/Electric/Petrol-default), `odometer_km = parsed(odometerKm)` else null, `is_full_tank = true` (matches the user's chip + odo defaults — they can change either on the dedicated EditFillup screen later); else falls through to the existing addExpenseWithItems path. Receipt-storage persistReceipt + golden-candidate capture + template-learning recordSample side-effects all stay on the same critical path regardless of the branch. - 2026-05-23 · OCR bugfix · SUPERMART / departmental receipts dropped all items at extraction — `META_KEYWORDS` includes `'hsn'` which feeds both `META_RE` (correct, used by `classifyRowWithContext` / `looksLikeMetaOnly` / `extractMerchant`) AND `SKIP_RE` (incorrect, used by every item extractor — `extractItemsTabular`, `extractItemsColumnar`, `extractItemsCard`, `extractItemsPharmacy`). Whenever ML Kit groups the `HSN.` column-label onto the item's y-baseline (which it does by default — `mergeIntoRows` in `parseReceipt.js:52` joins anything with ≥50% y-overlap), `SKIP_RE.test(l.text)` returns true and the row is dropped before `matchAmounts` runs → 0 items extracted, Scan Review opens with merchant/date/total prefilled but the items list empty and `needsReview=true`, user has to enter every line manually. Confirmed against `/home/akshat/Downloads/20260522_210218.heic` (SUPERMART, Noida — 2 items, ₹107) via host-side Node ESM harness (`/tmp/drift_ocr_test/`) that imports the app's actual `parseReceipt` + `patterns` + `detectFormat` + `columns` + `confidence` + `normalize` + `synonyms` (only `textRecognition.js` is stubbed — pre-extracted `rawLines[]` is passed in, the `Array.isArray(ocrResultOrLines)` branch in `parseReceipt.js` bypasses extractLines so the parser path under test is byte-identical to production). Fix: introduce `COLUMN_LABEL_META_KEYWORDS = new Set(['hsn', 'sac'])` in patterns.js and exclude them from the keyword list that builds `SKIP_RE`. They STAY in `META_KEYWORDS` so a standalone `HSN: 04039000` reference row is still classified as metadata (correct for totals/merchant detection / `findNameBackward` rejection) — only the item-extractor pre-filter is loosened. Restaurant receipts use `SAC` (Services Accounting Code, the GST sibling of HSN) as a per-row label the same way, so it's included symmetrically. Verified post-fix on 3 supermart variants: V1 (HSN inline — the realistic ML Kit grouping for this receipt) now extracts both items with correct qty/unit/price (Amul Masti Dahi 400g ₹35, Full Cream Milk 1000mL ₹72 — qty normalized via pack-count × pack-size which is intentional canonical-unit behaviour); V2 (HSN on its own y-track) and V3 (HSN absent) unchanged. Regression check against `/home/akshat/Downloads/20260519_213509.heic` (MANOJ HOUSE mandi, Noida — 7 produce items, NET ₹286) — format detection still hits `mandi` at confidence 1.0 (all 5 signature tests fire), all 7 items extract with `unit='kg'` via the `deriveQtyFromRate` weight-snap path (apple-kinnu 0.615kg @₹240, guava-white 0.135kg @₹120, shimla 0.180kg, roma 0.135kg, potato-pah 0.785kg, cucumber-h 0.705kg, tomato-hb 0.560kg), merchant/date/total all correct. The mandi receipt carries no HSN tokens, so the narrowing is a strict no-op for it; the regression check confirms this empirically rather than by argument. Known follow-ons (NOT fixed in this pass, per Rule 9 — keep the touch surface tight): (1) when HSN sits inline on the item row the columnar extractor preserves the bare `HSN.` token in the item name (e.g. `"AMUL MASTI DAHI HSN."`) because it doesn't match the qty-int (`^\d{1,2}$`) or weight-decimal (`^\d{0,2}\.\d{1,3}$`) regexes that consume other interstitial columns — cosmetic, user can edit in Review; future polish is to strip `\bHSN\.?\s*$` from the joined name in `extractItemsColumnar`/`extractItemsTabular`. (2) `ORDER_ID_RE` / `INVOICE_NO_RE` match `INVOICE\nPAYTM` across newlines because `[:\s#]*` consumes `\n` as whitespace — captures `"PAYTM"` as `orderId`/`invoiceNumber`. Out-of-scope for this fix; logged here for follow-up. Files touched (1): `app/src/ocr/patterns.js` (added `COLUMN_LABEL_META_KEYWORDS` set + `META_KEYWORDS.filter()` in the SKIP_RE composition + 6-line explanatory comment block above the constant). No schema migration, no new dependency, no native rebuild, no Babel/alias change. No commit. Phase totals unchanged (147/187 = 78.61%).

## Decision log

Record any binding decisions taken during execution (per Rule 8 — explain impact). Format:

```
- 2026-MM-DD · <decision> · <rationale> · <impact>
```

- 2026-05-17 · QW-01 scope kept minimal (try/finally only; no `db.closeAsync()` on schema error, no inline error logging) · Rule 9 — keep refactors tight; defensive close is opportunistic and `logError` helper is task 1.9 · Impact: if `execAsync(SCHEMA)` throws after `openDatabaseAsync` succeeds, the opened connection is dropped and GC'd by expo-sqlite rather than explicitly closed. No leak; minor untidiness on retry. Reconsider if a real-world failure shows up.
- 2026-05-17 · QW-02 took the `crypto.randomUUID()` + fallback path rather than literal `useRef` · The shorthand "→ useRef" in quick_wins.md cannot be applied to module-level exported `emptyRow()` / `rowsFromExisting()` without changing their signatures and updating 3+ external callers (scope creep for an XS task). The deeper recommendation in architecture_recommendations.md Improvement 17 actually proposes the random/UUID approach · Impact: keys now globally unique across hot-reloads and parallel screen mounts; no external callers affected.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-03 non-produce branch routed via `parsed.format` rather than "first non-grocery pot" or narrower fixes · Leverages existing format-detection investment (`detectFormat.js`) and matches the 11 declared formats; produce-heavy override preserves the original grocer-first instinct for any-format receipts · Impact: receipts now land in the correct category on first scan for fuel, pharmacy, restaurant, food-delivery, departmental, quick-commerce, utility, online-retail; generic/handwritten still fall back to `pots[0]`. If user renames default categories, matcher misses silently and falls back — better than the prior always-grocery behavior.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-04 PNG re-encode deferred to task 4.11 (`lightPreprocess`) rather than installing `expo-image-manipulator` now · Avoids Rule-1 dep additions in an XS quick-win; 4.11 will add resize + CLAHE + deskew + PNG in one place · Impact: only the `quality: 1.0` half of the spec lands today. Picked images are uncompressed but remain in source format (JPEG from camera, mixed from gallery). 4.11 closes the PNG gap.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-04 limited to `Scan.js` only; Add.js / EditExpense.js untouched · Those screens have no `ImagePicker` calls today — the tracker listed them anticipatorily. Wiring receipt-image attach into Add/EditExpense is real scope (storage, save-path) and belongs in task 5.12 (receipt image pipeline) · Impact: no change to Add/EditExpense UX; QW-04 closes cleanly with the two call sites that exist.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-05 included `wal_autocheckpoint = 1000` even though it isn't in the QW-05 one-liner · The dual-referenced task 1.3 lists all six PRAGMAs; splitting the single PRAGMA block across two edits would have been churn · Impact: closes QW-05 and 1.3 together. WAL files now self-checkpoint every 1000 pages — bounds sidecar growth and avoids the unbounded-WAL pathology on long sessions.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-07 took the direct-manifest-edit path instead of also adding `android.blockedPermissions` to `app.json` · User selected the simpler option after seeing both tradeoffs; `app.json` already lists only the 3 desired perms so prebuild was not the leak source — the extras were inherited from older library manifest versions at the original prebuild run · Impact: if `expo prebuild --clean` is ever re-run from current deps, the three removed perms should not regenerate; if it ever DOES re-add them, follow up with `blockedPermissions`.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-08 noted `expo-camera` dep + plugin remains in app.json/package.json despite zero source-level use · QW-08 / 1.13 spec asked to "confirm expo-camera need", not remove it. Removing it is its own task because (a) the plugin block contributes manifest entries other deps may rely on, (b) Rule 9 — don't bundle unrelated removals · Impact: ~2 MB bundle bloat still present; flag as a follow-up cleanup task before the production cut.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-09 kept `\p{N}` (numbers) in the normalized name despite the upstream regex spec saying "letters+whitespace" · The class also catches digits that survive SKU/qty stripping (e.g. brand names like "7up" or "5 Star"); `singularise()` is Latin-only so non-Latin scripts are untouched by the suffix pass; NFC done before lowercase to avoid casing inconsistencies on combining marks · Impact: brand-name digits no longer collapsed to whitespace; combined NFC + Unicode class recovers the entire Devanagari/Tamil receipt corpus.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-10 kept the babel `@core` alias work for later (task 2.1) · Relative `../core/domain/...` paths are slightly longer to read but require zero tooling changes; 2.1 will sweep them when it lands · Impact: no Babel/Metro reconfiguration risk in this batch; the alias swap will be a future find-replace pass.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-10 also left old `idx_expenses_category` in place alongside the new `idx_exp_cat_date` per the user-selected QW-11 option · See the related QW-11 decision; documenting here so a future "redundant index audit" task knows both decisions landed together.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-11 kept old `idx_expenses_category` despite being subsumed by the new composite under leftmost-prefix · User-selected option; the per-insert overhead is ~1 page; explicit DROP belongs in a dedicated cleanup task with full query-plan verification · Impact: writes carry a tiny redundant index cost until the cleanup task; reads are unaffected (SQLite picks the better index).
- 2026-05-18 · QW-12 chose a custom Reanimated banner over `ToastAndroid` · Cross-platform consistency, themeable via the same `F` palette every screen uses, and the host serves as the substrate for future Phase-2 UX items (2.D.07 swipe-to-delete, 2.D.09 undo snackbar). The `ToastProvider` sits inside `AppProvider` so it reads theme; outside `Navigation` so it floats above every screen · Impact: ~80 lines of new component code; no new deps. Banner uses `pointerEvents="none"` so it never intercepts taps. Re-firing replaces the prior toast in-place.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-18 scope kept tight: helper + only the two confirmed silent-swallow sites · The "replace empty catch {} across screens" framing in `quick_wins.md` implied broader sweep, but a `grep` across `app/src` found exactly one true `catch {}` (`Home.js:26`) and one near-empty (`ItemRows.js:121` swallowed `setSuggestions([])`). Every other try/catch in the app already has a real body (mostly `Alert.alert`). Avoided churning files that don't need changes per Rule 9 · Impact: developer-only signal in Metro; release builds tree-shake the `__DEV__` branch. Future task 1.9-style sweeps (when surface grows) can plug into the same helper.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-19 also moved `tabBar={(props) => <CustomTabBar {...props}/>}` into a module-constant `renderTabBar` · Wrapping `CustomTabBar` in `React.memo` while the parent re-creates the wrapping arrow each render is functionally pointless — the wrapper changes identity even when state/F don't. Module-constant fixes that and matches React-Navigation's documented "stable function reference" recommendation. The remaining concern — `CustomTabBar`'s `useApp()` call still subscribes the bar to every theme/state mutation — is by design; the memo only avoids re-render when nothing in `state` or `useApp()` changed · Impact: no behavioural change; perf gain measurable only with profiler. The constant declaration moved below `CustomTabBar` so reading the file top-to-bottom is straightforward.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-20 ErrorBoundary uses a static fallback palette (not `F` from `useApp()`) · The boundary must continue working if `AppProvider` itself crashed (e.g. DB open failure), so it cannot read context. Hard-coded the Flow cream/coral/ink tokens directly into the fallback view. Class component (not hook) because React 19 still requires `componentDidCatch` to live on a class · Impact: fallback UI looks correct in light mode but doesn't theme-switch in dark mode. Acceptable trade-off for crash-time resilience. Dev builds also print `componentStack` to Metro for diagnosis.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-20 per-screen boundaries applied via `withBoundary(name, Comp)` HOC at module top of `navigation/index.js`, not by editing each screen file · Keeps the screen files free of boundary-awareness, makes the wrapping audit trivial (one file lists every boundary), and avoids `displayName`/StrictMode surprises that would come from inline `component={withBoundary('X', X)}` per route (creates a new component identity each render → screen tree remounts on every nav re-render) · Impact: 20 screens now isolated; a crash inside Trends shows the fallback there while Home keeps working. The outer `<ErrorBoundary name="Drift">` in App.js catches anything outside `Navigation` (typically AppProvider boot errors).
- 2026-05-18 · QW-17 derived last-used category from in-memory `expenses[0].category_id` rather than persisting a settings key · Zero schema change, zero new write path, and reflects ACTUAL most-recent usage even after edits/deletes — a settings-table mirror would diverge whenever the user edits or deletes their last expense. Validates category still exists in current `pots` before defaulting (handles the "deleted my last category" edge case), then falls back to `pots[0]` · Impact: first session for a brand-new user defaults to `pots[0]` (no expenses yet) — same as before. Cost: a single `useMemo` on `[expenses, pots]` runs on Add mount; negligible.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-15 removed the header right "Save" link entirely (replaced with a 48px spacer for symmetry) instead of leaving both header and bottom · Two save affordances would compete for attention; quick_wins.md item #18 says "primary action gets primary visual weight". Bottom CTA in both modes now shows the live amount (`Save · ₹X.XX`) so the user gets running feedback that the header link never offered. Quick-mode keypad's safe-area `paddingBottom` collapsed since the CTA bar below it now owns the bottom inset · Impact: single source of truth for save; CTA always visible above keyboard since it sits below the keypad and the keypad is the height-fixed sibling of the ScrollView.
- 2026-05-18 · QW-14 Add removed from Tab.Screen list entirely (not kept as a placeholder route) · The cleaner alternative was a stub Tab.Screen that auto-navigates to the modal stack, but that leaves a phantom route in `state.routes` that we'd then need to skip in the tab bar — circular. Better: synthesise the `+` button as a static cell at index 2 of the visual row, sandwiched between `state.routes[1]` (Scan) and `state.routes[2]` (Trends). `navigation.getParent()?.navigate('Add')` is the standard React-Navigation pattern for tab→stack hops · Impact: tabs are now Home/Scan/[+]/Trends/Subs (5 visual cells, 4 actual routes + 1 static). The Tab.Navigator initial route is unchanged (Home). Saving in Add still calls `navigation.goBack()`, which now pops the modal back to whatever tab the user came from (previously: switched to the Add tab).
- 2026-05-18 · QW-13 chose `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` over a custom JS picker · Standard Expo-supported native module, ~zero maintenance, opens the OS-native date dialog (already familiar to users). Display mode forked: `'inline'` on iOS (recommended by the package), `'default'` (modal-spinner) on Android. Date stored as `YYYY-MM-DD` string for compatibility with existing parser output and `expenses.expense_date` schema. `maximumDate=new Date()` prevents future-dated receipts. Requires an `expo prebuild` re-run for the native module to link · Impact: one new native dep; if the user later swaps to managed Expo, the package is auto-supported. No app.json plugin block needed for prebuild workflow.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.1 / 1.2 legacy-stamp path used instead of forcing every existing user through v1.up · The current schema is a fully idempotent `CREATE IF NOT EXISTS` blob, but re-running it on a populated DB would land an `applied_at = now` row that lies about the install age. Probing `sqlite_master` for all 10 v1 tables (`V1_REQUIRED_TABLES`) before stamping handles partial-install corner cases — if even one table is missing, we let v1.up run and its `IF NOT EXISTS` guards prevent any conflict · Impact: user's existing install on `Drift.apk` boots into v1 with zero DDL re-run, audit trail honest. Phase 1.C (3.1+) lands as v2..v14 on top of the stamp.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.1 / 1.2 `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` lifted out of the v1.up DDL blob into the runtime PRAGMA block in `db/index.js` · `foreign_keys` is a per-connection setting (resets to OFF on every new connection) — not DDL. Leaving it inside v1.up meant it ran exactly once at v1 install and never again; the `expo-sqlite` connection probably re-enabled it via its own defaults, but the contract was wrong. Now it's set on every `openDatabaseAsync`, before any migration runs · Impact: every connection now actually enforces FKs (especially `expenses.category_id → categories(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` and `receipt_items.expense_id → expenses(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`). No behavioural regression if expo-sqlite was already setting it; defence-in-depth if it wasn't.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.6 sort + cap helper (`sortExpenses`) used after every patch instead of always prepending · The SQL `ORDER BY expense_date DESC, created_at DESC, id DESC` is the canonical order; a back-dated expense (scanned today, dated last week) prepended would visibly jump to the top of AllExpenses until next refresh — a bug visible to the user. Sorting + slicing back to `EXPENSES_LIMIT=500` on every patch keeps the in-memory array byte-identical to what `expRepo.list({ limit: 500 })` would return · Impact: O(n log n) on a 500-row array per mutation — sub-ms on any device. The cap stays at 500 until 8.1 ships FlatList virtualisation, then it can drop.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.7 `pots` shape preserved via in-memory `rowToPot` mapper rather than touching the 8 consumer screens · `summaryByCategory` returns `spent` (SQL aggregate convention), and screens read `pot.spend` / `pot.key` / `pot.label`. Mapping at the boundary in `useAppState` keeps the diff to one file and respects Rule 9 (no churn beyond the task). The canonical shape (drop the aliases, use `spent` everywhere) becomes a follow-up cleanup task once any other Phase-1 work is touching those screens · Impact: zero screen changes, identical render output, identical rounding (`+(spent\|\|0).toFixed(2)` matches the previous `+(byCat.get(...)\|\|0).toFixed(2)`).
- 2026-05-18 · 1.6 / 1.7 every category mutation now also calls `refreshSummary()` even though categories aren't part of 1.6's scope · `pots` are derived from `categories` joined to expense aggregates; adding a category, changing a budget, or renaming/recoloring one all change the pots payload. Without the refresh, `Home`/`Trends` would stay stale until the user pulled-to-refresh. Cost is one tiny aggregate query · Impact: category list and pots stay in lockstep. Removing a category still leaves stale `category_id` on in-memory expenses (FK SET NULL is async-from-the-client's-pov); the next `expRepo.list()` reload (refresh or app restart) repairs it. Acceptable — the orphan rows don't show in pots anyway.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.10 keystore stored at `~/.drift/drift-release.jks` (outside the repo) and credentials at `app/android/keystore.properties` (inside the gitignored `/android` tree) — not in `~/.gradle/gradle.properties` as the execution_roadmap.md:233 risk note literally suggested · `~/.gradle/gradle.properties` is process-wide for the user and any other Android project would inherit `storeFile` / `keyAlias` keys, which is the inverse of "secrets that belong to this app". `keystore.properties` adjacent to `build.gradle` is the documented React Native pattern, sits behind two layers of .gitignore (the project's `/android` rule and a defensive explicit `keystore.properties` line), and uses `chmod 600` for filesystem-level isolation. Functionally equivalent for credential safety; ergonomically much better for a solo-developer single-app repo · Impact: a fresh clone with no `keystore.properties` produces a debug-signed release APK + a WARNING line at Gradle config time. That's a friendly default for local dev but means CI/release pipelines must explicitly verify the WARNING is absent before publishing.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.10 keystore is PKCS12 (not JKS) · keytool emits a literal "It is recommended to migrate to PKCS12" warning on JKS creation as of JDK 21; PKCS12 is the industry standard cross-platform format and Gradle reads both interchangeably. Regenerated as PKCS12 from the start rather than running the post-hoc `keytool -importkeystore` migration · Impact: zero behavioural difference for signing; keystore is portable to any toolchain (apksigner, jarsigner, OpenSSL) without conversion.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.10 release build falls back to **debug** signing (not failure) when `keystore.properties` is missing · A hard-fail would block local dev for anyone who clones the repo without the secret. Soft-fall + loud WARNING preserves the QW-08-era ability to run `assembleRelease` for size-checking on any clean clone. The trade-off: a release pipeline that ignores the WARNING could ship a debug-signed APK; documented in `android-signing.md` as the explicit pre-publish check · Impact: net-positive for solo dev workflow; release-pipeline hygiene now relies on an apksigner verify step (also documented).
- 2026-05-18 · 1.11 `shrinkResources` left disabled even though R8 minify is on · Resource shrinking can drop drawables/strings that Reanimated or RN reflectively load (image-picker placeholders, font assets, etc.), and Drift currently has zero UI regression coverage. Once 2.D.* polish tasks land + we have visual snapshot or device-walkthrough automation, flip `android.enableShrinkResourcesInReleaseBuilds=true` and measure the additional savings · Impact: ~1-3 MB of potential APK savings deferred; safety net intact.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.11 proguard-rules.pro covers every dep currently on the classpath, including ones (`expo-camera`) the source no longer uses · Removing keep rules for soon-to-be-evicted deps would couple this task to QW-08's "confirm expo-camera need" follow-up. Cleaner: keep them now, drop them in the same PR that removes the dep. No size or correctness cost (R8 only removes classes that are unreferenced AND lack keep rules; keeping a rule for an absent class is a no-op) · Impact: zero. The keep set is a superset of what's strictly needed and survives any single dep removal without re-edit.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.11 release-APK size dropped 143MB → 138MB (3.5%) with minify on, not the 30%+ commonly cited for R8 · Drift's bytecode payload is small (one screen-set, no analytics SDKs, no Firebase, etc.) — the bulk is native libs across 4 ABIs (Hermes ~25MB×4, ML Kit text recognition models ~12MB, expo-sqlite native ~4MB, Reanimated turbomodule ~3MB×4). R8 only shrinks Java/Kotlin code, and that's the small slice of the pie · Impact: the bigger size wins are 8.6 (WebP receipt thumbnails) and ABI splits (split APKs per arch, deferring 3 of the 4 ABIs from each APK). Note this so we don't chase R8-as-a-size-tool further in Phase 1.

Sets the template for migrating other AppContext-only-for-F components in future cleanup.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.11 chose the roadmap-literal taxonomy with `home` / `trends` / `scan` as standalone features (not folded into `items` or `analytics`) · Matches the 8-feature list 2.9 names verbatim + 3 dashboard/orchestration features that own one screen each. - 2026-05-18 · 2.9 chose sibling providers + pure-aggregator `useApp()` hook (no shim AppContext) over a single-Provider-with-internal-contexts split · The hook-aggregator path is the cleanest extensible end state: every screen that migrates off `useApp()` onto direct `useExpenses()` / `useCategories()` subscribes only to its slice and avoids the all-state re-render. - 2026-05-18 · 2.9 cross-feature edge: `pots` live in `ExpensesProvider`, not `CategoriesProvider` · The SQL summary query is `SELECT c.id, ..., SUM(e.amount) FROM categories LEFT JOIN expenses ... GROUP BY c.id` — primarily an expenses aggregate keyed on category. Putting pots in Categories would mean every expense mutation has to call into a hook outside its own provider; putting them in Expenses means category mutations call `useExpenses().refreshSummary` after their own DB write, which is the simpler dependency direction (Categories sits inside Expenses in the provider tree so it can read `useExpenses()`) · Impact: `useApp().pots` (and `useExpenses().pots`) both work and return identical data. CategoriesProvider returns just the `categories` rows; pots-as-derived-view is owned by the provider that owns the underlying summary state.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.9 introduced a small `RefreshBus` context (`core/state/RefreshBus.js`) instead of passing `refresh` callbacks down through props · `resetApp` and pull-to-refresh need to fan out across every slice; without a bus, each provider would have to expose its `refresh` upward through a prop or a deep callback chain. Ref-based registry sidesteps re-render churn (no state inside the bus) and lets providers self-register on mount via `useRegisterRefresh(key, fn)` — the entire pattern is ~30 lines · Impact: testable in isolation, mock-able for future tests, and the bus also doubles as the hook point for any cross-feature event (e.g. "user opened the app from background" maintenance triggers per 8.7).
- 2026-05-18 · 2.10 exposed read-only repo methods on the providers via arrow wrappers (`netWorth: (...a) => accRepo.netWorth(...a)`) instead of binding methods directly · `items.topMover()` internally calls `this.trackedItems()` — exposing `topMover: itemRepo.topMover` as a bare reference loses the `this` binding and crashes on call. When 8.9 lands the cache logic generalises straight into the shared `useQuery` with no contract change for Home.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.14 cache eviction signal is `expenses[0]?.id` (top-of-list), not `expenses.length` or a full hash · Adding an expense or editing the most-recent one changes the head row's id; deleting the most-recent does the same (id of new head differs). For the rare back-dated insert that doesn't touch the head, the cache stays warm until the user pull-to-refreshes — netWorth/streak/topMover are insensitive to back-dated expenses anyway since they're per-month/lifetime aggregates, not strictly-most-recent. The cheaper signal beats the precise one here · Impact: zero false-positive invalidations during scroll/tab-switch (which is the whole point of the cache); one corner case (back-dated insert without refresh) tolerates ≤ 30 s stale dashboard values.

Ref-based latest-fetchers gives `load` a stable identity (empty dep array) while still calling the freshest fetcher · Impact: the version effect fires exactly when `expenses[0]?.id` changes — not on every parent render. - 2026-05-18 · 2.17 used `function X + export default React.memo(X)` rather than the inline `const X = React.memo(function X(){})` form QW-19 used for primitives · Inline form is denser but it forces the memo wrapper to be the only export — fine for primitives, but several screens have inner helpers (`FilterPill` in AllExpenses, `Section` in NetWorth, `ManageList`/`EditOne` in EditPot, `ItemEditor` in Scan, `Field`+`input` in EditSub, `Field`+`inputStyle`+`chipStyle` in EditTrip, `tripLengthDays` in Travel, `Row` in Profile) that share file scope with the default export. Two-line `function X(...); export default React.memo(X);` keeps the helper layout untouched and gives DevTools a named-function display (`X`, not `Anonymous`) · Impact: 21 mechanical rewrites, zero structural change to any of the 8 files with internal helpers. The diff for each is exactly two edits (param list rename + EOF append).
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 batched as 10 separate migration objects (v2..v11), one per task ID, not collapsed into a single mega-v2 · User-selected. Keeps each version independently stamped in `schema_version`, mirrors the v1 baseline pattern, and lets a future failure pinpoint exactly which migration broke. Cost: ~10 trivial objects with `version`, `name`, `up`. Benefit: per-version recoverability and a clean audit trail · Impact: schema.js gains ~180 lines (SQL constants + migration objects) but stays readable; the runner change for v10 (see below) is the only structural addition.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1 deleted_at columns landed without partial indexes (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`) · The execution_roadmap and tracker spec list no such index. Adding one preemptively would (a) bloat every write path with an extra index maintenance entry for a predicate that's currently never queried, (b) cement a query pattern (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` only) that 3.15's repo rewrite may end up using differently (e.g. range-predicates with month_key + a covering index). Defer to a profiler-driven decision in 3.15 · Impact: soft-delete column reads cost the same as any nullable column. If list queries grow slow once 3.15 adds the predicate, a partial index can be added in its own migration cheaply.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.2 month_key chose VIRTUAL over STORED · User-selected. Existing-row migration is O(1) (column metadata only, no row rewrite), index works the same either way (SQLite ≥ 3.31 supports indexes on both kinds). The cost is recompute-on-read for non-indexed paths, but every query that uses `month_key` will hit `idx_exp_month` or `idx_exp_month_cat`, so the compute happens at index-build time, not at query time · Impact: migration completes instantly on a 50k-row table; storage unchanged; STORED would have doubled the row-size penalty of the column data with no read benefit on indexed queries.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.3 / 3.4 merchants and products tables kept to the minimal v1 shape (id, name, canonical_name, created_at) · User-selected. The FK from `expenses.merchant_id` / `receipt_items.product_id` remains valid regardless of future column adds · Impact: smallest possible surface area to introduce these tables; future migrations will ADD COLUMN incrementally as features land — no rebuild needed.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.4 `products_fts` FTS5 table created without sync triggers · Sync triggers (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on products → products_fts) live with 3.13's overall FTS hookup work (which also covers `item_fts` over receipt_items). Creating the table now without triggers means it stays empty until 3.13 or until callers manually push rows via `INSERT INTO products_fts(rowid, name, canonical_name) VALUES (...)` — verified working in the dry-run · Impact: 3.4 lands the schema; 3.13 lands the wiring. No queries break because no consumer references products_fts yet.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.5 nullable FK columns declared with explicit `REFERENCES … ON DELETE SET NULL` rather than plain INTEGER · User-selected. `db/index.js` already enforces FKs via `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, so the declarations actually take effect — deleting an account/trip/subscription will null the column on associated expenses instead of orphaning. Application-only integrity (plain INTEGER) loses that guarantee for the sake of marginally cheaper writes · Impact: write-time FK lookups add a tiny per-row cost (negligible for ≤100k rows), reads unchanged. If a future bulk-import wants to skip FK enforcement, it can `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` within a transactionless migration the same way v10 does.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.5 `expenses.currency` default = NULL, not 'INR' · User-selected. NULL = "inherit profile/settings.currency at read time"; a literal default would have backfilled every historical expense with whatever the current setting is, baking that decision permanently. With NULL, a future settings change retroactively reinterprets the historical view (which is what most users want; the alternative requires a UI to retro-fix every row). `amount_home` and `fx_rate` follow the same NULL default — historical rows have only `amount` and that's read as-is · Impact: queries must `COALESCE(currency, (SELECT currency FROM settings))` to surface display currency; minor extra cost vs. column-only read. The complexity buys correctness on currency-switch.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.7 / 3.8 `account_transactions` and `goal_contributions` include nullable `expense_id` link (FK ON DELETE SET NULL) · User-selected. Lets the ledger tables optionally point back to the originating expense for drill-through (e.g. "this account debit came from the Zomato expense"). ON DELETE SET NULL means deleting the expense doesn't lose the ledger row — important because the ledger is the source of truth for account balance reconciliation. The link is optional, so externally-recorded transactions (e.g. interest, bank fees) stay supported · Impact: enables a Phase-2 "view source expense" affordance without forcing it; ledger tables remain valid even when the originating expense is purged.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 runner extension: added `transactionless: true` flag instead of carving v10 out as a special case · User-selected. SQLite's `PRAGMA foreign_keys` cannot toggle inside a multi-statement transaction (the runtime ignores the pragma if a transaction is open), so the v10 table rebuild needs autocommit context. The minimally invasive fix is a 15-line `if (mig.transactionless) { ... } else { withTransactionAsync(...) }` branch in `runMigrations()`: the migration owns its own transaction discipline, the runner stamps schema_version after the migration's COMMIT. The migration must be idempotent on retry (stamp insert can still fail after the rebuild succeeded) — handled by `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS subscriptions_new` at the top of v10 and an `INSERT … SELECT` that works whether subscriptions has the old or new shape · Impact: future rebuilds (e.g. when a Phase-3 task adds a new CHECK constraint to expenses) reuse the same flag with no further runner change. Stamping race documented in `db/index.js` comment.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 verdict value coercion: rows with `verdict NOT IN ('keep','cancel','review')` silently rewritten to 'keep' during INSERT … SELECT, not rejected · User-selected (implicit by approval of the plan). Failing the migration on bad data would brick the app for any user whose subscriptions table has a verdict outside the new enum — and since v1 had no CHECK, any string is technically valid pre-v10. Coercing to 'keep' (the safe default) preserves the row and the user can re-categorise from the Subs UI. Dry-run confirmed the coercion path with a seeded `verdict='pause'` row · Impact: data preservation > strict validation. Worst case: a user who deliberately typed 'pause' for "consider pausing" loses that bookkeeping. Acceptable since the Subs UI has no such option and the value would have been from a debug session.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 subscriptions_new uses a hand-rolled column list rather than `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT * FROM subscriptions` · The AS-SELECT shortcut drops constraints, defaults, and FKs (it just copies type-affinity + data). Hand-rolling the column list lets us redeclare every constraint (NOT NULL defaults, the new CHECK, the new linked_category_id FK) and matches the SQLite docs' official table-alteration recipe verbatim · Impact: schema integrity preserved across the rebuild; the post-migration `PRAGMA table_info('subscriptions')` shows all expected columns with their constraints, verified in the dry-run.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.10 `budget_period` declared NOT NULL with DEFAULT 'month' · The CHECK applies to all rows including historical (existing rows get the default value at ADD-COLUMN time per SQLite semantics). NOT NULL ensures future inserts can't bypass the CHECK with NULL. The three allowed values match the Phase-4 rollover budgets work (7.10) which will need monthly/weekly/yearly cadence selection. Defaulting to 'month' preserves all existing budget-tracking behavior · Impact: zero behavioural change today (the column is unused); future budget rollover work has a typed cadence to read from without retro-migrating every category row.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 V1_REQUIRED_TABLES intentionally left frozen at v1's 10 tables · Adding the new tables (merchants, products, account_transactions, goal_contributions) here would break legacy-stamp detection on installs created before v2 existed — those installs would no longer match the "all v1 tables present" check and would fall through to v1.up (safe, but unnecessary churn). The TABLES export (used by `resetAll()`) DID get the new tables, in children-first order so resetAll FK cascades stay valid · Impact: legacy-stamp logic stays correct across all future migrations; only TABLES grows with each new table. Comment added in schema.js to lock this convention.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 deferred application to next user-initiated app launch (validation done via node:sqlite, not by running the app) · User-selected. The migration ladder was dry-run against an in-memory SQLite seeded with a verdict='pause' row + an expense linked via subscription_id, exercising the v10 FK-survival and verdict-coercion paths. Running against the real `drift.db` is the user's call — backing it up before next launch is recommended (and noted in the implementation summary) · Impact: zero risk of mid-session corruption from this session; user retains full control over when migrations apply.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 chose `category_id INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` sentinel over `category_id NULLABLE` in `monthly_summary` · SQLite allows multiple NULLs in a PRIMARY KEY (historical bug, never fixed), so `PRIMARY KEY (month_key, category_id)` with NULL category_ids would not enforce one row per (month, uncategorised) bucket — every NULL-category expense would create a new row. AUTOINCREMENT starts at 1 so id=0 is a safe sentinel for uncategorised. Triggers `COALESCE(NEW.category_id, 0)` everywhere they map expense→bucket · Impact: aggregations group correctly; consumers (3.19's summaryByCategory) join via the same COALESCE on c.id which never returns 0 (real categories start at 1), so uncategorised rolls into the LEFT JOIN's "no match" branch and contributes only via `(0,…)` rollup rows. If we later add a synthetic "Uncategorised" category in the categories table, it'd have id ≥ 1 and the rollup behaviour stays consistent.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 split v12/v13/v14/v15 across 4 migrations instead of one mega-v12 · Mirrors the v2..v11 batching convention from 3.1–3.10. Each migration is independently stamped in `schema_version`; a future failure pinpoints exactly which artefact broke (rollups vs expense FTS vs item FTS vs backfill). v15 is the only one whose semantics differ on first-run (no-op on fresh) vs upgrade (one-shot populate from existing data) · Impact: schema.js grows by ~330 LOC; the 4 stamps make the audit trail honest.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 item_summary AU trigger handles the OLD.normalized_name == NEW.normalized_name same-bucket case via decrement-then-UPSERT-increment, which transiently passes points_count=0 and can DELETE the row before the UPSERT re-creates it · Traced via dry-run: when N=1 (only row for that name), step 1 decrements to 0, step 2 deletes the row, step 3's recompute UPDATE is a no-op, step 4 INSERT-ON-CONFLICT becomes a plain INSERT seeded from NEW.* with `points_count = 1`. End state is correct (1 row, NEW values). When N>1, step 2 doesn't fire, step 3 recomputes last_* from remaining live rows (which now include the post-UPDATE NEW row), step 4 ON-CONFLICT increments. End state is also correct · Impact: trigger is correct in all transitions but transiently inconsistent within the trigger body itself. Safe because triggers are atomic with their host statement — no other reader can observe the intermediate state.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 / 3.19 monthly_summary rollup is soft-delete-aware → no `deleted_at IS NULL` predicate on the rollup side in 3.19's rewrites · Triggers maintain the invariant: every row contributing to `monthly_summary.total` has `expenses.deleted_at IS NULL`. Filtering on the rollup would be a double-negative no-op. Confirmed by the dry-run: `SUM(monthly_summary.total)` exactly equals `SUM(expenses.amount WHERE deleted_at IS NULL)` after each mutation · Impact: simpler queries in `summaryByCategory` / `monthlyTrend` / `trackedItems` / `suggest`. If a future task introduces a "view deleted" surface that wants to see soft-deleted aggregates, it would need a parallel rollup or a different query path — flagged as a constraint for the eventual Recycle Bin feature.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 FTS5 triggers evict soft-deleted rows (user-selected) instead of leaving them indexed · Consistent with the rollup choice. The trade-off: search results only surface live data, which means a future Recycle Bin can't reuse the FTS index for soft-deleted entries — it'd need either a separate FTS view or to drop the predicate temporarily. Acceptable for the current scope where soft-delete is not yet user-visible · Impact: AD triggers do `INSERT INTO expense_fts(expense_fts, rowid, ...) VALUES ('delete', ...)`; AU does delete-old + insert-new gated on each side's deleted_at. When a row is revived (deleted_at NULL → NULL), AU re-inserts the FTS row.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.17 deviated from "rewritten with FIRST_VALUE() OVER" to "read directly from item_summary" · The spec was written before item_summary existed (drafted in execution_roadmap.md which lists 3.11 separately from 3.17). With item_summary now storing `display_name`, `kind`, `last_unit`, `last_unit_price`, `last_canonical_unit`, `last_seen` per `normalized_name`, FIRST_VALUE OVER `receipt_items` would re-derive what's already materialised. The end goal — "no correlated subqueries" — is satisfied more directly · Impact: suggest() is now a single indexed PK-prefix lookup (`WHERE normalized_name LIKE 'x%'` uses item_summary's PK index when the pattern is a literal prefix). Strictly faster than the old query and the FIRST_VALUE alternative. The FIRST_VALUE form is preserved in the v15 backfill SQL, so the pattern is documented in the codebase for future reuse.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.16 kept `total_qty_30d` as a per-row live query against `receipt_items` instead of materialising into item_summary · The 30-day window is sliding (today-relative); a stored value would be stale within hours and require either a daily maintenance job to refresh or every read to invalidate. Live per-row aggregate is acceptable because trackedItems is a screen-load operation (not a list-render hot path) and the 30-day filter uses `idx_items_name_date` (already exists) · Impact: same number of queries as before (one main + N per-row spark + N per-row 30d). Future task can pre-materialise into a `rolling_30d` column if profiling shows it matters; today's data scale (<10k items) makes it not worth the complexity.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.C.20 helper-only path (no ESLint) per user-selected option · Codebase has no `.eslintrc*` / `eslint.config.*` and no `eslint` dep today. Adding ESLint just to enforce one rule would be 30-50 lines of config + custom-rule scaffolding + new devDep — more weight than the safety it buys when the helper is a one-line copy-paste at every list query. The 4 exported predicate constants from `db/predicates.js` document the intent in-place and pattern-match for code review · Impact: a missing predicate in a future list query is a code-review catch, not a CI catch. If predicate drift becomes a real problem (>1 incident), the file is structured for an ESLint rule to be added later without further refactor — the constants are the regex anchor.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.C.20 by-id `get(id)` paths intentionally left unfiltered · The literal spec said "every list query"; `get(id)` is a single-row primary-key lookup, not a list. Filtering it would either silently return null for soft-deleted IDs (subtle UX regression — "where did my expense go?") or require every caller to handle both shapes. Today no mutation path writes `deleted_at`, so the question is moot, but the conservative choice preserves the current behaviour. The flip can land alongside whichever task introduces user-facing soft-delete (Recycle Bin / Undo) · Impact: `get(id)` and `update(id, patch)` will still resolve soft-deleted rows until that future task arrives. Acceptable because no current code soft-deletes anything.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.5 plausibility window set to `year ∈ [2000, currentYear+1]` rather than [1990, currentYear] · A `+1`-year head-room covers the common end-of-year reality: a Dec 31 receipt clocked by the camera as Jan 1 next year (timezone drift, or photo metadata after midnight). Pre-2000 rejected because Drift's user base is on smartphones; there are no 1990s receipts being scanned · Impact: years 2027 (when this lands; today's currentYear+1) and beyond will continue to be accepted as the system clock rolls forward — the window auto-advances. A receipt with a typo'd year of 2099 still falls through to the orchestrator's fallback (today's date).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.5 walk-every-match strategy in `findDate` rather than first-match-only · An OCR string like "Bill: 32/13/2026 ... GSTIN ... 15 May 2026" should land on `15 May 2026`. The previous first-match-only code would have returned `null` from the bad `32/13/2026` and then never tried `MONTH_DATE_RE`. With the `g`-flag walk, both regexes are exhausted before falling back. Cost: bounded by the number of date-shaped fragments per receipt (typically 1–3) · Impact: poison-pill date strings no longer derail a perfectly extractable receipt date.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.6 trailing-price threshold = 0.7 (last 30% of the line) rather than the more literal "amount must be the rightmost token" · An exact-rightmost rule would mis-classify "Total ₹1,234.50" (1 amount, trivially OK) but also mis-classify multi-amount lines like "CGST @9% ₹45.00" if interpreted strictly. The "last 30%" heuristic handles both correctly: with one amount, the function short-circuits to trailing=true; with multiple amounts, only the last one's *position* matters. 30% specifically because most receipt rows put labels on the left and amounts hard-aligned to the right · Impact: bill rows where the keyword sits in the middle of a longer free-text label ("Total Mix Snack 4 x 50 200") correctly classify as `item`. Edge case: a very short label like "Tax 45" (4 chars before amount) — 4/8 = 0.5, less than 0.7, would classify as `item`. Acceptable because the corresponding TAX bottom-band detection in `parseReceipt`'s fallback path still recovers the total via reconciliation.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.6 `meta` test moved BEFORE the trailing-price gate · "Order ID: ABC123" has no amounts and a mid-string price-position would be undefined; testing META_RE first short-circuits cleanly and keeps `meta` behaviour identical to pre-rename. Same for `bill_header` — those never carry amounts · Impact: zero classification change for meta / bill_header rows. The new rule only affects the 5 amount-bearing categories.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.7 5% RELATIVE tolerance rather than absolute · The previous code used `Math.abs(d - round(d)) < 0.05` which is absolute — that breaks down for fractional snaps (a value of 0.30 is 0.05 absolute from 0.25, accepting it would wrongly snap). Relative tolerance scales with the target: 5% of 0.25 = 0.0125 (so 0.30 correctly rejected), 5% of 50 = 2.5 (so 50.5 correctly snaps to 50). The implementation: `Math.abs(derived - target) / target <= 0.05` · Impact: integer behaviour is essentially the same as before (5% of 1 = 0.05, identical bound); fractional behaviour is strict, no over-snap regression.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.7 fraction set = `{0.25, 0.5, 0.75}` only (no thirds, no eighths) · User-selected. Indian retail receipts deal almost exclusively in halves and quarters (half-kg sugar, quarter-litre milk). Thirds (1/3 = 0.333) would create ambiguity with OCR'd 0.30 (real qty); eighths are vanishingly rare. Pharmacy split-strip cases that genuinely need 1/3 will continue to surface as the raw derived value (no snap, qtyHint=null → tabular extractor falls through to the trailing-1-2-digit-integer pickup) · Impact: cleaner predictability. If thirds become a real signal later, the FRACTIONAL_SNAPS array is a one-line extension.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 seed data smaller (~5KB each) than the tracker's `~80KB / ~50KB` estimates · The bytes estimate in execution_roadmap.md was speculative; in practice 150 Hinglish staples + 95 Devanagari entries is enough to cover the daily-staples corpus that drives most repeat-purchase analytics. Brand-tail (Amul Gold, Mother Dairy products), pharmacy (drug names), and regional vegetables (Tamil/Telugu/Bengali equivalents) are the natural growth axes — they should land in their own content PRs with translator review, not crammed into this scaffolding task · Impact: schema/loader contract is finalised; the JSON files double in size painlessly as content PRs grow. No code change needed when content grows.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 whole-string lookup ONLY (no substring/word-substitution) · Substring substitution would silently rewrite parts of compound names: "Amul Doodh 1L" → "Amul milk" — which loses the brand context that price-history-per-merchant relies on. Whole-string lookup is conservative: only exact matches (after NFC + lowercase) are canonicalised, everything else passes through unchanged. Brand+staple combos like "Mother Dairy Doodh" stay distinct from the bare "Doodh" → "milk" canonical · Impact: predictable, no surprise renames. The user can add a `"amul doodh": "milk"` entry to the JSON if they specifically want that collapse for a given brand+staple pair — explicit > implicit.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 `canonicalizeName` chains Hindi → synonyms (two-stage) rather than a single flat map · Some entries chain through Devanagari → Hinglish (already in hindi_product_map) → English staple (caught by product_synonyms). For example: `दूध` → `milk` is a one-shot; but if a future entry maps `दूध फुल क्रीम` → `doodh full cream` (Devanagari → Hinglish) and `doodh full cream` is already in synonyms (→ `milk`), the two-stage pipeline catches both. Each map can be authored against the alphabet its translator knows best · Impact: content PRs can add Devanagari entries that point to existing Hinglish keys without needing English coverage. Saves duplicate maintenance.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 ScanService passes a separate `taxInvoice` state object rather than spreading every field as a top-level state · Five new useState calls in Scan.js would have crowded the existing 11 hooks past the threshold where the component is comprehensible. A single `taxInvoice = { gstin, invoice_number, cgst, sgst, igst }` slice spreads cleanly into `addExpenseWithItems({ expense: {...base, ...(taxInvoice||{}) }})` and is null-able in one place for resetScreen. Pattern matches how `parsed` carries those fields end-to-end · Impact: zero render perf difference (1 useState vs 5 + spread); cleaner reset path; trivial to add a "Tax details" expansion section in 5.11.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 CGST/SGST/IGST split into 3 columns rather than a `tax_breakdown` JSON column · User-selected.
