The chosen path is (b): default = computed (works for the 90% of users who never look at the formula closely), `emi_override` available for users who reconcile statements. The schedule's last-installment-adjusts-to-zero rule mops up the cumulative drift regardless of which value is used. EditEMI surfaces "Computed ₹X · drift +₹Y" so the user knows exactly what their override is doing. · Impact: no special-casing per bank, no rounding-mode config. Risk register line 842 explicitly called this out as the right mitigation. If a user complains "my schedule is off by ₹5 vs my statement", the fix is one field on EditEMI. The override doesn't change the underlying math (interest still computed from balance × monthly_rate); it only changes the payment amount used to derive principal_paid each month.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 `bill_day` CHECK capped at 28 (not 31) · The Feb-29/30/31 edge case in date math is annoying. A loan billed on the 31st would have to "fall back to the 28th" every February, then "jump back to the 31st" in March, then "fall back to the 30th" in April, etc. Three options: (a) allow 1–31, anchor-day-clamp at runtime (same approach as 7.2's SubCalendar projection); (b) cap at 28 in the schema CHECK; (c) cap at 28 in the schema AND clamp at runtime (defence in depth). Chosen path: (b) — schema CHECK rejects 29/30/31 at INSERT time so impossible states can't exist; the amortization helper still clamps with `Math.min(anchorDay, daysInMonth)` as defence in depth. Real-world Indian bank EMIs always bill on 1st/5th/10th/15th/20th/25th anyway — capping at 28 covers every realistic case without forcing the user to think about "what if the month is short". The 1–28 range is also what subscription billing systems converge on (Netflix, Spotify, etc.). · Impact: data is clean by construction. If a future user with an "EMI bills on the 30th" loan shows up (rare; banks would have to support the same edge case in their own systems), the fix is a one-line schema migration to widen the CHECK + relying on the existing anchor-day clamp in the amortization helper.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 EMI lives under `features/emi/`, not `features/accounts/`, despite loans being liabilities · The existing `accounts` table has `kind = 'asset' | 'liability'`, so conceptually a loan could be modelled as a liability account. Two arguments for keeping EMI separate: (1) the amortization-heavy UX (schedule preview, per-installment rendering, override field) has zero overlap with the existing account-tracking UX (just a balance number); folding EMI under accounts would either bloat the accounts repo with EMI-specific methods or fork accounts into "simple" and "EMI" subtypes — both worse than a clean separation. (2) the FK from `expenses.emi_loan_id → emi_loans.id` mirrors `expenses.account_id → accounts.id` (both ON DELETE SET NULL, both partial-indexed on non-null); having two parallel parents is the right shape since they answer different questions ("which account paid this?" vs "which loan does this installment service?"). A user can have both — pay an EMI from their HDFC current account, and the expense links to both the account AND the loan. · Impact: clean module boundary. The cost is one extra provider in the tree (EmiProvider next to SubsProvider). If a future task wants a unified "Money" tab listing accounts + subs + EMIs, the consolidation happens at the screen layer (the Hub already has rows for each), not the data layer. The 7.5 master-roadmap-line-831 "EMI sits under a future Money tab" comment is preserved as a future option without forcing it now.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 EMI link picker landed on EditExpense only, not Add · The Add screen is already dense after 7.3 (TagChipSurface) — adding another picker would push the visual rhythm and make first-time users wade through "did I want a tag? a payment method? an EMI link?" on every quick-spend entry. The chosen scope: EditExpense gets the EMI chip surface; users who pay their EMI as a fresh expense log it normally and then tap the row to add the EMI link in Edit. Two reasons this is OK: (a) Add's primary workflow is "I just spent ₹X at Y, log it before I forget" — pure speed; EMI linkage is reflective work; (b) the chip surface only renders when `emiLoans.length > 0`, so users without any loans never see it anywhere. If user research later shows the link is wanted at log-time, adding the same `<TagChipSurface>`-style picker to Add is ~20 lines. · Impact: Add stays lean. EditExpense becomes the canonical "fix-up" surface for every secondary metadata (tags, payment method, mood, recurring, EMI link, notes). The chip-row idiom is now consistent — both tags and EMIs use the same horizontal-scroll-with-toggle pattern.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 schedule preview shows next 3 installments on EditEMI, not the full schedule · A full N-month schedule rendered inline would be 240 rows for a typical home loan — visually overwhelming on EditEMI and irrelevant for the form's purpose (verifying the numbers work, not browsing the schedule). The chosen UX: a "SCHEDULE PREVIEW" card showing the computed EMI + total interest + total paid + the next 3 installments (using `installments_paid` to pick the slice). Gives instant feedback as the user types — change the rate, see the EMI update; change `installments_paid`, see the next-3 list shift. Full schedule browsing is a future feature (probably a dedicated "EMI detail" screen reached from the list); EditEMI is for entering/correcting the terms. · Impact: the EditEMI screen stays focused on data entry. The 3-row preview is enough to catch obvious typos (a ₹50L principal with a 30-month tenure produces EMIs over ₹1L — the preview makes that wrong before the save). If a user wants to see the full amortization, that's a future task — the `buildSchedule()` helper is already exported and used by the preview, so the screen would just render the full array.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 prepayments + variable rate explicitly OUT of scope for v1 · Real loans get prepaid (₹1L lump sum reduces principal, schedule recomputes); real Indian home loans are RBI-repo-rate-linked floaters whose rate changes mid-tenure. Both are common; both add significant scope. Step 2 Q4 explicitly framed three options including these; user chose "fixed rate, no prepayments". The implementation reflects this: no `prepayments TEXT` JSON column, no `rate_history TEXT` column, no rate-change-aware schedule recomputation. The `emi_override` field provides a coarse escape hatch — if a user actually does prepay or experience a rate change, they can manually update `principal` (after a prepayment lands) or `annual_rate_pct` (after a rate revision) and the schedule re-derives from the new terms. Lossy (doesn't preserve prepayment history) but workable for v1. · Impact: 7.5 ships in ~5 hours instead of ~10. The lossy "edit the loan terms" workaround means a user who prepays loses the audit trail of which installments were prepaid, but the outstanding balance + remaining schedule will be correct going forward. Future polish (probably 7.5b) can add `prepayments TEXT` JSON storage + rate-history; the existing `amortization.js` would need to accept those arrays and walk the schedule accordingly. Schema migration would be additive — no existing column changes.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 `installments_paid` is a user-controlled field, not derived from linked expenses · A "smart" design would derive `installments_paid` from `COUNT(expenses WHERE emi_loan_id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL)` at read time. Then "paid this month?" auto-updates as the user logs payments. Three reasons the manual field is right: (1) the linked-expense count and the formula-derived schedule answer different questions — the count says "how many bank debits I've tagged" while the schedule says "how many installments my loan has progressed through"; they should be allowed to diverge (e.g. user prepaid 2 installments at once but only logged one expense, or has 3 months of arrears); (2) mid-loan setup is the common case — when a user adds an existing loan that's already at installment #7, they enter `installments_paid = 7` once and the schedule + outstanding are correct without back-filling 7 expense rows; (3) the "X of N paid · Y linked" sub-line on the EMI list makes both numbers visible, so any drift is obvious and easy to fix manually. · Impact: the EMI list shows two complementary signals — the user's stated progress (installments_paid) and the actual logged debits (linkedCount). A user who wants them in sync can update either; a user who doesn't care still sees correct outstanding/nextDueDate from the manual field. The simpler "derive from links" would have made the EMI list wrong-by-default for anyone who didn't log every single EMI as an expense (which is most users).
- 2026-05-23 · OCR bugfix narrowed `SKIP_RE` rather than removing `'hsn'` from `META_KEYWORDS` · The minimal-touch fix is to take `'hsn'` and `'sac'` out of META_KEYWORDS entirely. Rejected: `META_RE` (and thus `looksLikeMetaOnly`) is the same union and a STANDALONE row like `"HSN."` would then become eligible as an item-name backward-search hit in `findNameBackward` (`length ≥ 3` ✓, `/[a-z]/i.test` ✓, `looksLikeQtyOnly` returns false because no digits/unit-words remain after cleanup — would return the bare `"HSN."` as a name). Same row would also become a merchant-candidate in `extractMerchant` for receipts where HSN appears in the top 25%. By keeping the keywords in `META_KEYWORDS` and only filtering them out of the `SKIP_RE` composition, classification stays correct everywhere except the one place that was wrong (the item-extractor pre-filter). `'sac'` is included symmetrically because GST restaurant receipts use SAC the same way HSN is used on goods receipts — and `'sac'` already shipped in `META_KEYWORDS` alongside `'hsn'` since the original meta-row design (the pair is treated as one concept). · Impact: the surface area of the change is 1 file, ~10 lines including comment. `META_RE`, `looksLikeMetaOnly`, `extractMerchant`, `findNameBackward`, `classifyRowWithContext` are byte-identical in behaviour for both the SUPERMART and MANOJ HOUSE inputs. The ONLY observable behavioural delta from this fix is: item-extractor `SKIP_RE.test()` returns false for rows containing inline `HSN`/`SAC` labels — which is exactly the bug being fixed.
- 2026-05-23 · OCR bugfix validated via host-side parser harness rather than on-device ML Kit · ML Kit's `recognize(uri)` is a native Android-only call and the project has no CI/test runner. The fix touches pure-JS parser logic (`patterns.js`'s `SKIP_RE` composition), not OCR itself. A Node ESM harness at `/tmp/drift_ocr_test/` was built that imports the app's real `parseReceipt.js` + dependencies and feeds in pre-built `rawLines[]` with realistic per-token `elements` (x/width position metadata so `detectColumns` actually fires the columnar path that the bug is most visible in). The parser path under test is byte-identical to production from `mergeIntoRows` through `dispatchItems` through `scoreConfidence`. Per the CLAUDE.md "validation pattern for pure-function helpers" convention, this is the right level of test for a regex-keyword change. · Impact: the fix has been verified against the SUPERMART receipt (now extracts 2 items + ₹107 total) and the MANOJ HOUSE mandi receipt (unchanged 7 items + ₹286 total). The harness was NOT committed (per the same CLAUDE.md convention — validation harnesses live in `/tmp/`). If a regression suite is later established, this is exactly the shape it would take: synthetic `rawLines[]` derived from real receipt photos + asserted expectations on the parser output. Future maintainer reading this: re-create the harness by copying `app/src/ocr/{parseReceipt, patterns, detectFormat, columns, confidence, textRecognition}.js` + `app/src/core/domain/{produce, units, normalize, date, synonyms, *.json}` into a `/tmp/` ESM project, stub `textRecognition.js` to a no-op `extractLines`, and feed `parseReceipt` an array of merged-line objects.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.7 auto-populate gating uses `item_summary.points_count >= 2` rather than `kind in (produce, grocer)` or "every grocery scan" · The user asked Q1 at Step 2 with three options. Aggressive auto-populate (every grocer/produce row) was rejected because it fills the pantry with one-shot purchases ("a bottle of fish sauce I bought once for a recipe") that the user does not actually own a continuing inventory of. Manual-only was rejected because it loses the core "pantry that fills itself from your receipts" value prop. The chosen `points_count >= 2` gate aligns with the existing `reorderQueue()` analytic (same threshold) so the two surfaces tell a consistent story: an item is "in the user's life" once they've bought it twice. The signal is maintained automatically by the v12 AI trigger on `receipt_items` that increments `item_summary.points_count` — no parallel counter to keep in sync. · Impact: the pantry stays curated by default — a fresh user adds 5 grocery scans and only items that recur (rice, milk, eggs, dahi) materialise in the pantry; one-shot purchases (saffron, balsamic, mango pickle) don't. The user can still add anything manually for one-shots they want to track (EditPantryItem create flow). The "second purchase triggers tracking" rule does mean there's a one-receipt lag before a new staple appears — acceptable for the noise reduction it buys. Future polish (7.7b) could expose the gating threshold as a setting; not needed in v1.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.7 quantity stored as canonical units rather than packs/states · Q2 framed three options. Canonical units + numeric current_qty (chosen) matches how `item_summary.canonical_unit` already represents quantity — "500 g" / "1000 ml" / "12 pcs" — so the auto-populate code can directly use the canonical_qty already on receipt_items without conversion. Whole-units-only was rejected on fidelity grounds (forces "1 dahi" when the user actually has 250 g of dahi left in a 500 g tub). Three-state (full/low/empty) was rejected by the roadmap phrasing ("depletion UX" implies a continuous qty). · Impact: the stepper UX uses a 0.1 step for weight/volume units and 1 for pcs, which keeps the per-tap delta natural; the "Used up" shortcut sets current_qty=0 explicitly when the user finishes the bottle. The shopping list query is a single SQL comparison (`current_qty <= reorder_threshold`) covered by the partial index. Threshold semantics are also numeric — "fire when below 200 g" makes sense and is testable with a hard number. The downside is the user has to think in canonical units for thresholds — a Future polish (7.7b) could offer a pack-aware threshold UI ("when fewer than 2 packs"), but for v1 the existing pre-filled candidate-list flow (which copies canonical_unit from item_summary at creation) shields the user from the units choice for auto-populated rows.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.7 low-stock notification fires on threshold cross with ISO-week dedupe rather than cadence-based · Q4 framed three options. Threshold-based (chosen) is the simplest model that matches the chosen quantity model: current_qty is the source of truth, threshold is the trip wire. Cadence-based (the existing `reorderQueue()` math) was rejected because (a) it operates on a different signal (purchase cadence vs. inventory level) and would conflict with the user-set threshold, (b) it needs at least 3 distinct purchase dates to compute, so brand-new pantry rows would silently never fire, (c) it's already exposed in the Reorder Queue screen for users who prefer that lens. The "both" hybrid was rejected as a noisier compromise without a clear UX win. Dedupe key `pantry:<normalized_name>:YYYY-Www` (ISO-8601 week) was chosen over `YYYY-MM-DD` so a notification re-arms automatically the next Monday if the user still hasn't restocked, but doesn't spam on intra-week mutations (e.g. the user keeps tapping the - button to log consumption). · Impact: any pantry row with a non-NULL `reorder_threshold > 0` is in scope; rows with NULL threshold (the default for auto-populated rows — chosen at Q3) are silently skipped. The user explicitly opts in by setting a threshold on EditPantryItem. This is conservative: no false fires on rows the user hasn't configured. The flip side: the user has to set a threshold to get any low-stock alerts. The EditPantryItem screen's threshold sub-text explains exactly this. The `evaluatePantryLowStock` pure function lives alongside `evaluateBudgetThresholds` / `evaluateSubsDue` / `evaluatePriceAlerts` so the boot evaluation + notifications-enabled-flip evaluation + on-mutation evaluation paths all stay symmetric across feature checkers.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.7 NotifyBus gains a new `PANTRY_CHANGED` event vs. piggybacking on EXPENSE_CHANGED · The auto-populate path runs on every expense save, so `EXPENSE_CHANGED` already triggers a pantry re-evaluation. But manual mutations on the Pantry screen (stepper, "Used up", "Bought", create, update, delete) don't touch expenses — without a dedicated event the low-stock checker would only re-run when the user happened to log an expense afterward. Adding `PANTRY_CHANGED` is one event constant + one listener registration; cheaper than the alternative (always-poll, or "evaluate on every render" anti-pattern). · Impact: each PantryProvider mutation emits PANTRY_CHANGED and NotificationsProvider's listener re-evaluates the low-stock checker. Reliably fires the notification within a tick of the user crossing the threshold via the stepper. Cost: two NotifyBus events touch the same code path per scan-driven mutation (EXPENSE_CHANGED from ExpensesProvider, PANTRY_CHANGED from PantryProvider when the auto-populate refresh lands). The dedupe key gate inside `notification_log` (the UNIQUE index on dedupe_key) makes the duplicate evaluation a no-op — at most one INSERT per (item, week). Acceptable.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.6 `fuel_fillups.expense_id` is `NOT NULL UNIQUE` (1-to-1) rather than `NULL`-able or a column on `expenses` · Three modelling options were on the table during Step 2. Option A (chosen): every fill-up has exactly one expense, deleting either cascades to the other. Option B: NULL-able expense_id — lets a fill-up exist without a money row (gift fuel, logbook backfill). Option C: drop the table entirely and put `vehicle_id` + `liters` + `rate` + `odometer_km` directly on `expenses`. Option B was rejected because Drift's core model is "every rupee tracked through expenses" — a fill-up without an expense would leak from Pots/Trends and create a per-feature reconciliation problem. Option C was rejected by the task tracker phrasing ("`vehicles` + `fuel_fillups` tables") and on architectural grounds: it bloats `expenses` with N mostly-NULL fuel-only columns (liters/rate/odometer/full-tank/fuel-type) that scale linearly with future fuel tracking sophistication, blocking option (A) ever becoming attractive again. · Impact: a fuel receipt today produces two rows in one transaction; deleting the expense from AllExpenses fires the FK CASCADE which wipes the fillup automatically (no separate cleanup code path); the fill-up table grows independently of `expenses` so future per-fillup metadata (loyalty points, station GST, fuel grade) lands on `fuel_fillups` rather than further bloating `expenses`. The UNIQUE constraint is load-bearing — without it, the dual-write transaction could be invoked twice for the same expense row and produce orphaned fillups; the constraint moves that invariant to the DB layer.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.6 `fuel_fillups.amount` is denormalised (mirrors `expenses.amount`) rather than computed via JOIN · The per-vehicle hero card needs "this month's fuel spend" for each vehicle row. The straightforward shape is `SELECT SUM(e.amount) FROM fuel_fillups f JOIN expenses e ON e.id = f.expense_id WHERE f.vehicle_id = ? AND substr(e.expense_date, 1, 7) = ? AND e.deleted_at IS NULL AND f.deleted_at IS NULL`. The denormalised shape is `SELECT SUM(amount) FROM fuel_fillups WHERE vehicle_id = ? AND substr(fill_date, 1, 7) = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL`. The latter avoids a JOIN, hits `idx_fillups_vehicle_date` directly (partial index already covers the deleted_at filter), and stays correct as long as the application writes both rows inside the same transaction — which is exactly what `fillupsRepo.createWithExpense` guarantees. The risk is drift: an `expenses.amount` UPDATE that doesn't also update the fillup would diverge. `fillupsRepo.updatePair` patches both sides; soft-delete is symmetric; resetAll respects both. · Impact: aggregatesByVehicle returns in O(log N) per vehicle instead of O(N) per fillup × JOIN. For 100k+ transactions across a 10-year horizon, this is the difference between an unnoticeable query and a perceptible lag on the Vehicles list. The drift risk is bounded to "user edited the expense.amount via EditExpense without going through EditFillup" — possible, but rare in practice (most fuel-row edits go through EditFillup), and visible to the user immediately (the Vehicles list would show wrong this-month spend). A future polish (7.6b) can add an UPDATE trigger on `expenses` that auto-syncs `fuel_fillups.amount` when the linked expense's amount changes; skipped here to keep the touch surface tight (Rule 9). For now: the application boundary is the single source of truth, just like `monthly_summary` is for category totals.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.6 vehicle picker on Scan review screen rather than post-save prompt · Step 2 Q4 framed three placement options. Auto-pick at scan time (chosen) puts the chip on the existing review screen, defaults to the last-used vehicle (or vehicles[0] when no fill-up history exists), and surfaces a "None" pill so the user can opt out and save as a normal expense. Post-save prompt (rejected) saves the expense first, then opens a modal/toast offering "Log fill-up" — adds a confirmation step and an extra navigation hop; degrades to "I have to remember to tap Yes" when the user is in a hurry. Always-auto-create (rejected) is the "Drift never assumes" rule applied: it falls apart with 0 vehicles or 2+ vehicles, and even with 1 vehicle there are scenarios where the user has the wrong vehicle defaulted (e.g. spouse's bike). · Impact: 90% of fuel scans land directly into the dual-write path with zero extra taps. The 10% that need attention surface a clear "None" pill and a visible chip strip — both are 1 tap away from correction. The auto-pick uses `lastVehicleUsed()` which queries a single row from `fuel_fillups ORDER BY fill_date DESC LIMIT 1` — partial-index-covered and effectively free. Falling through to `addExpenseWithItems` when no fillup is wanted means the normal expenses pipeline is unchanged for non-fuel scans and for users who don't care about per-vehicle tracking. Compared to 7.5's pattern (EMI link chip on EditExpense post-save), 7.6's at-scan-time chip is faster but requires the chip to be present on the review screen — fine, it's only rendered when `format === 'fuel'` so non-fuel scans are visually unchanged.
- 2026-05-23 · 7.6 mileage = full-tank-to-full-tank odometer delta, not per-fill-up · Real-world fuel-economy math has two modeling choices. Option A (chosen): compute mileage from the latest two `is_full_tank = 1 AND odometer_km IS NOT NULL` fillups, treat their odometer delta as "km driven on the latest fillup's tank". Option B: sum liters across all fillups in a window, divide into the odometer delta from the window's first to last reading. Option A is the "fuelly.com" convention and is interpretable per fill-up. Option B smooths over partial fills but requires the user to consistently log odometer at every fill, which is unrealistic. With Option A, partial fills are visible to the user (as "partial" badge) but skipped by the mileage window — so a user who occasionally tops up doesn't poison their mileage stat. The full-tank `Switch` defaults ON because that's the common case for the average user. · Impact: VehicleDetail's hero card shows kmpl as soon as the user has logged two full-tank fills with odometer (the practical minimum for any mileage calculation). Users who log without odometer see "—" with no error state — they can backfill or accept that mileage is unavailable. The pure-SQL `mileageWindow()` query degrades gracefully when fewer than 2 qualifying rows exist (returns null) — the screen handles null cleanly with "—". Future polish (7.6b) could expose a per-vehicle "rolling 5 fillups" alternative; not needed in v1 — most users will trust the latest-two reading.
- 2026-05-21 · 7.5 on-device UI verification deferred · The validation harness covers schema + FK + amortization math comprehensively — 51/51 pass across the migration ladder, schema shape, FK SET NULL behaviour, textbook EMI snapshots (₹1L @ 12% × 12 = ₹8884.88), Indian home loan snapshot (₹50L @ 8.5% × 240 ≈ ₹43391.16), zero-rate fallback, invalid inputs, schedule closes-to-zero invariant, override path with last-installment compensation, projectState across mid-loan/new-loan/fully-paid states, and tenureLabel formatting. What it can't cover: actual rendering of the EMI list with hero + progress bar + "X linked" sub-line + per-row "next YYYY-MM-DD" timestamp, EditEMI form layout with the 8-icon and 8-colour picker grids, the live SCHEDULE PREVIEW card updating as inputs change (a real UX check — does the preview feel snappy?), the "Computed ₹X · drift +₹Y" override hint copy, the EMI link chip row on EditExpense gating on `emiLoans.length > 0`, SwipeableRow swipe-delete + Undo Toast sequence, the Hub Reports + Profile entries, dynamic title swap on EditEMI route between "Add EMI" and "Edit EMI". Per CLAUDE.md — listed as an 8-item punch list. · Impact: the SQL + amortization math is correct against schema + pure-function contracts. UI correctness against the React Native renderer has not been observed by me. The screens use the same primitives (`View` / `Text` / `TouchableOpacity` / `ScrollView` / SwipeableRow / Toast / Alert) and theme tokens as existing surfaces (Subs list, EditSub form, TagChipSurface), so rendering risk is concentrated in the EditEMI form's density and the schedule preview's update responsiveness — easy to spot-fix.

---

## Notes on resuming a session

When you re-engage with me (Claude) on this project after a break, do:

1. **Read `/docs/10-final/task_tracker.md`** (this file) first.
2. **Scan for `[/]` (in progress) and `[!]` (blocked)** lines — those tell you where I stopped mid-flow.
3. **Check the "Currently active" line** under the legend — it points to the active task.
4. **Read any new entries in the Completion log and Decision log** to understand what changed.
5. **Confirm with the user** which task to start or resume before touching code (Rule 1, Rule 15).

This file is the durable contract. The in-tool TaskList is session-scoped and is rebuilt per session from this file.
